[1] Ghoul L21
Its exact appearance is not known, because later, "Khephren the Great, who raised the Second Pyramid, carved over the Sphinx’s face in the likeness of his own." In 1910, the magician Harry Houdini glimpsed the Unknown God of the Dead after being imprisoned in an unknown temple hundreds of feet below the Gizeh plateau, and perhaps constituting an undiscovered lower level of the Temple of the Sphinx near the Second Pyramind. The thing was being worshipped by hybrid men with the heads of hippos or crocodiles, along with King Khephren (aka the guide Abdul Reis el Drogman) and the ghoul-queen Nitokris. As it emerged from a huge dark doorway, the Unknown One appeared at first to be something yellowish and hairy, about the size of a hippopotamus, with five heads from which curious rigid tentacles extended to seize large offerings of carrion. Soon after, Houdini realized that this creature was the merest forepaw of a much larger being, presumably with an overall form resembling the Sphinx. --- 1._The_August_Derleth_Special_--_A_man_inherits_a_house_which_once_belonged_to_a_wizard_and_he_gets_f71eeb08ee.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L47
1. The August Derleth Special -- A man inherits a house which once belonged to a wizard and he gets possessed by the spirit of his evil ancestor. 2. The Deep One Denial -- a man with fishy features eventually transforms into a Deep One. 3. The Tome of Terror -- a guy finds a book (or other object) and raises a monster which eats him. 4. Dunwich Doldrums -- A monster is released and ravages the countryside. 5. Ghoulish Grandparents -- A man starts hanging out with Ghouls and becomes one. 6. Cthulhu Phone Calls -- Guy is being driven crazy by Cthulhu's calling and goes on a long quest and finds him and goes nuts when he does. 7. Revolting Revenge -- Bad guys pick on the wrong guy who sends squidgy terror to kill them. 8. Body Transfer Troubles -- A guy steals the body of another person. There are probably others I have forgotten but this is a good list to avoid.


--- A Creak in the Floor.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L55
Synopsis When Charlie, a young man from out of town, is invited by his old friend Pete to come stay with him for a while, he finds the home an enormous warehouse-like slum inhabited by dozens of colourful characters, and his friend missing. Throughout a series of conversations with the residents, Charlie realises that the majority consensus between them is that Pete and his girlfriend had uncovered a nest of ghouls living beneath the streets and were killed by them. Unnerved by this story, he nevertheless follows one of the tenants to the expansive basement when she tells him she knows where Pete is. As shapes begin to stir through a hole in the basement wall, she explains that the stories are in fact true, and that Charlie himself now poses a problem to the creatures he has seen... Characters Charlie , a young man looking for a new start in a new town. Dally , a resident who appears to function as something of a superintendent for the building.

[2] Ghoul L71

--- A Photograph From Life.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L77
Price . It was published in the Matthew Carpenter -edited thematic anthology Pickman's Gallery by Ulthar Press in 2018. Synopsis Detective Thomas Malone is assigned to investigate the disappearance of the eccentric Bostonian artist Richard Upton Pickman . After interviewing his associate Thurber (whose first name Price gives as Ethan) the two take a trip to Pickman's studio, and the two investigate a suspicious well in the basement. Traversing the seemingly-endless stairs contained within, they find themselves in the Dreamlands , and quickly happen upon Pickman himself. For an unexplained reason, Pickman transforms himself into a ghoul and kills Thurber, though Malone manages to escape and presumably make his way back to the waking world. Behind the Mythos Thomas Malone is possibly the character created by H. P. Lovecraft for his 1927 short story " The Horror at Red Hook ", although this is never confirmed. If this is the case, whether the story takes place before or after the events described in "The Horror at Red Hook" is uncertain, although it seems likely to be the latter.

--- Agents of Dreamland.txt ---

[2] Ghoul L87
They use alien spores and messages sent via TV snow to turn themselves into the seeds of a fungal apocalypse. Characters The Signalman An agent of Albany and an alcoholic. Born in 1960, he is 55 at the time of the story but looks at least ten years older. Gets his nickname from the silver railroad-style pocketwatch he carries. Known as "the one who never flinched". Has an apartment in the Santa Monica Hills. Immacolata Sexton: An agent of Y. The Signalman refers to her as a "ghoul dressed up like a woman". She is active for well over a century, and experiences past, present, and future simultaneously. Jack Dunaway: A hated colleague of the Signalman, 40 years old. He was recruited out of MIT. Recognizable by his "ferrety eyes and crooked nose, the thin lips and jutting chin". Agent Vance: Another colleague of the Signalman. Becomes infected with the alien fungus seven days after exposure. Drew Standish: The leader of the Moonlight Ranch cult.


--- Aiueb Gnshal.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L63
McKinnon & Dylan K. Sharpe Aiueb Gnshal The Eyes Between Worlds ) is a fictional entity created by Kevin T. McKinnon and Dylan K. Sharpe for the Call of Cthulhu scenario "The Eyes Between Worlds", which was published in the Miskatonic University Library Association Monograph Strange Tales of Dread and Wonder Overview Aiueb Gnshal is a mysterious Lesser Other God who has made his home in a long-lost temple in Bhutan , as well as many other temples. He appears as a formless black void with seven orb-like eyes. It is mainly worshipped by Ghouls who worship him in a defiled cult spoken of in the Cambuluc Scrolls of the wizard Lang-Fu. Looking into the eyes of this horrid being, after a disgusting and awful ritual, will allow you to see into the Court of Azathoth , in which he resides simultaneously as he does across his temples. It is said that the Mongolian warlord Temujin, better known as Genghis Khan, had the favour of Aiueb Gnshal.


--- All Moon-Beasts Amorphous and Mephitic.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L59
НАЗВАНИЕ: All Moon-Beasts Amorphous and Mephitic ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:11:40 СИМВОЛОВ: 1685 All Moon-Beasts Amorphous and Mephitic Author Peter Cannon Language English Genre(s) Parody Fantasy Publication Crypt of Cthulhu Publication date "All Moon-Beasts Amorphous and Mephitic" is a comedy short story by Peter Cannon which features veterinarians James Herriot and Siegfried Farnon from the All Creatures Great and Small franchise setting up practice in H. P. Lovecraft Dreamlands . The story was first published in 1995 in the magazine Crypt of Cthulhu , and is also included in Cannon's 2011 collection Forever Azathoth: Parodies and Pastiches Synopsis James Herriot is a veterinary surgeon working in the Dreamlands who has treated all sorts of species, from Ghouls to Wamps . One day, King Kuranes calls him to look at his beloved Moon-beast , Matilda, a strong and healthy racer, now suddenly looking ill, with her pink tentacles turned to a sickly shade of green. Herriot prescribes an ointment, but with little success: a few days later, a distressed Kuranes appears in the clinic with Matilda looking worse than before. Herriot and fellow doctor Siegfried Farnon try to get the Moon-beast to consume the prescribed medicine by offering it inside the body of a tasty Zoog .


--- Amanda Sharpe.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L73
A 2-year student at Arkham 's prestigious Miskatonic University during the late 1920s, Amanda has been having nightmares of an ancient entity sleeping beneath the waves. These dreams were sparked by the sight of a painting hanging in the university’s halls, and have had a negative impact on her schoolwork. Ghouls of the Miskatonic In Graham McNeill 's novel Ghouls of the Miskatonic , Amanda and her friend and roommate Rita Young were kidnapped by a cult of Cthulhu worshippers who intended to feed the pair to their pet ghouls . Fortunately, the two were saved by an unlikely band of heroes, including Rex Murphy and Finn Edwards Behind the Mythos First introduced in Fantasy Flight's Arkham Horror Second Edition Core Set , Amanda Sharpe has since appeared in several other of the company's releases, including Eldritch Horror and Arkham Horror: The Card Game , and a plastic model of Amanda was created for the Arkham Horror Premium Figures series.

[3] Ghouls L153
The "Personal Story" mechanic introduced in the Innsmouth Horror expansion for Arkham Horror tells of how Amanda is possessed of Deep One heritage; it should be noted that no previous mention of this condition had been made. Although it is possible for her to transform fully into an aquatic form (automatically losing the player the game), it is also possible to prevent her transformation through gameplay. In Ghouls of the Miskatonic it was revealed that she was born in Muncie, Indiana, and was a friend and roommate of Rita Young at Miskatonic's Dorothy Upman Hall.

--- Avalon_ The County of Somerset.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L91
The Mythos elements described in Avalon tend heavily toward a focus on the Outer God Shub-Niggurath and her cults, and the Deep Ones , much of Somerset having been flooded in the (geologically) recent past. Scenarios Blood and Water Strange Little Girl ": after wrongly receiving a mysterious letter, the investigators look into the case of a missing scholar who has inadvertently roused the ire of the Dark Child, a spectral avatar of Shub-Niggurath herself. St. Swithun's Hole ": while spelunking in search of archaeological treasures, the investigators must fend off a band of ancient ghouls who have not seen the light of day in many centuries, while desperately searching for a way out of their predicament. Avalon: The County of Somerset was published in 2010 by third-party content provider Cubicle 7 Entertainment as part of their hugely popular Cthulhu Britannica series. Released in both print and digital PDF versions, it is currently out of print.

--- Bast (Cthulhu Universalis).txt ---

[1] Ghoul L39
The temples of the goddess were located in Bubastis and Elephantine. Cat-headed Bubastis frowns down as a sullen guardian in the purple pylons before the pyramids. The ghost of Bubastis lurks in desert ways. Myths of animal-headed deities such as Bast are allegories of forgotten truths. 6] For Egyptian gods and religions were based on secret realities. Strange hybrids walked the earth when it was young; gigantic, lumbering creatures—half-beast, half-human. The priests of Bubastis viewed their god as a representative of monstrous beast-men who shambled on Earth in primal days. They regarded Bast as carnivorous, as the ghoul-goddess, Chewer of Corpses. Prinn's Saracenic Rituals chapter records that the priests were blaspheming against the reigning religions and performing atrocious sacrifices. The beast-mouths of the gods hungered for blood. The priests had made strange and curious bargains with their divine Masters. The priests were mating animals and humans in an attempt to create a hybrid with the attributes of their deity. Unnatural perversions drove the cult of Bubastis out of Egypt. After Nephren-Ka , the new ruler and his people drove out the wicked priests who prostituted their faith to carnivorous Bubastis.

--- Bhutan.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L59
НАЗВАНИЕ: Bhutan ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:12:31 СИМВОЛОВ: 981 Kingdom of Bhutan Location South Asia Type Country The Kingdom of Bhutan is a mountainous landlocked country in South Asia, bordered by China to the north and India to the south . Although never referenced in H. P. Lovecraft 's writings, it has appeared in the Cthulhu Mythos work of other authors. The Call of Cthulhu Roleplaying Game In Chaosium Call of Cthulhu "Eyes Between the Worlds" scenario, Bhutan is home to a long-lost temple which houses the obscure Outer God Aiueb Gnshal and its cult of ghoul worshippers. The scenario was written by Kevin T. McKinnon and Dylan K. Sharpe, and included in the Miskatonic University Library Association Monograph supplement Strange Tales of Dread and Wonder in 2006. The CthulhuPunk Roleplaying Game In the Steve Jackson Games tabletop roleplaying game CthulhuPunk , Bhutan has been incorporated into the Chinese bloc as one of its satellite states, primarily to provide a buffer for China against its political and military opponents.

Characters Titus Crow In his first appearance, Crow is introduced as "a London-dwelling collector of obscure and eldritch volumes"--including a 400-year-old copy of the Cthäat Aquadingen , bound in human skin. He is engaged in unspecified "studying," largely nocturnal, because "he was better able to concentrate at night." He professes to "believe in ghosts and fairies, in ghouls and genies , in a certain mythological ‘prop’ [i. e. , the Necronomicon ], and in the existence of Atlantis R’lyeh , and G’harne ." Physically, he is described as tall and broad-shouldered and it was plain to see that in his younger days he had been a handsome man. Now, though, his hair had greyed and his eyes, though they were still bright and observant, bore the imprint of many a year spent exploring—and often, I guessed, discovering—along rarely trodden paths of mysterious and obscure learning. He was attired in a flame-red dressing-gown....


--- Black Rites.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L39
НАЗВАНИЕ: Black Rites ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:12:36 СИМВОЛОВ: 780 The Black Rites Created by Luveh-Keraphf Used by James Allington Type Occult text Real World Information Creator Robert Bloch First Appearance CIRCLE The Suicide in the Study Other Appearances CIRCLE The Grinning Ghoul The Black Rites are a fictional text invented by Robert Bloch , attributed by Bloch to his character Luveh-Keraphf , a thinly veiled allusion to Bloch's friend H. P. Lovecraft The Black Rites are first mentioned in Bloch’s " The Suicide in the Study " (1935), where a copy is owned by the wizard James Allington. They are described as being written by "the mad poet Luveh-Keraphf, priest of Bast The book comes up again in " The Grinning Ghoul " (1936), where Bloch refers to it as "the grotesque Black Rites of mystic Luveh-Keraph, the priest of cryptic Bast."

[2] Ghoul L71
P. Lovecraft The Black Rites are first mentioned in Bloch’s " The Suicide in the Study " (1935), where a copy is owned by the wizard James Allington. Моя кровь застыла, потому что я увидел две длинные, тощие руки, как гнилые конечности трупа, медленно устремившиеся к моему сжавшемуся от страха горлу. Монстр сел, и даже в когтях ужаса я различил смутное и ужасное сходство между существом в гробу и каким-то древним портретом в зале особняка. Две когтистые лапы, холодные, как пламя ледяного ада, сомкнулись вокруг моего горла, два глаза проникли, словно личинки, через мою обезумевшую сущность, смех, порожденный безумием, одиноко звучал в моих ушах, словно гром судьбы. Мир свернулся, обернутый туманом огненной смерти. Внезапно чары разрушились.

[10] Гуле L51
С громким воплем я кинул зажженную спичку на этот отвратительный труп и продолжал вопить, когда распахнул ворота и выбежал из гробницы в мир здравомыслия, оставив позади тлеющий огонь, из обугленного сердца которого страшный голос все еще слабо стонал, словно тоскливый реквием по тому, кто когда-то был Джереми Стрэнджем. Теперь гробница разрушена, а вместе с ней — лесные захоронения и все скрытые камеры, а так же уничтожены рукописи, которые служат напоминанием о жутком кровожадном гуле, что невозможно забыть. Ибо земля скрывает безумие и видения отвратительной реальности, а чудовищные твари пребывают в тени смерти, скрываясь и ожидая, чтобы схватить души тех, кто ввяжется в запретные дела. Примечания Примечания Симультанный (в психологии) (от лат. simul — в одно и то же время) — термин, означающий практическую одновременность протекания каких-либо психических процессов ввиду их свернутости и автоматизированности. Например, симультанным является смысловое восприятие устной речи на родном и иностранном языке переводчиком-синхронистом.


--- Bokrug.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L131
The deity sleeps beneath the calm waters of a lake that bordered Ib and the city of Sarnath In the Expanded Mythos In Brian Lumley 's " The Sister City " and Beneath the Moors , Bokrug is reinterpreted as a species, rather than a single deity. According to these tales, the Bokrug came to colonise Earth in ancient times, bringing along the voiceless Thuun'ha who worshiped them. The Bokrug life cycle is similar to that of the Ghouls , as they send their children to the surface world to be raised by humans. Like Deep One hybrids, the Bokrug look mostly human until they reach a point in their lives in which they start mutating and develop an urge to abandon the human world and rejoin their own species.


--- Caitlin R. Kiernan.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L79
Our smallness and insignificance in the universe at large. In all possible universes. Within the concept of infinity. No one and nothing cares for us. No one’s watching out for us. To me, that’s Lovecraft. They also cite John Steinbeck as a "tremendous formative influence," who "taught me invaluable lessons about characterization; that stories, novels, are not about events. They’re about people.... Steinbeck also introduced me to the importance of profoundly flawed characters." Other writers who play important roles in Kiernan's fiction include Lewis Carroll and Charles Fort. Kiernan's novels Threshold (2001), Low Red Moon (2003), and especially Daughter of Hounds (2007) explore the subterranean world of ghouls as envisioned in Lovecraft's " Pickman's Model ". Their Tinfoil Dossier series-- Agents of Dreamland (2017), Black Helicopters (2018), and The Tindalos Asset (2020)--mix the Cthulhu Mythos with the shadowy world of government conspiracies, a la The X-Files Kiernan's 2019 collection Houses Under the Sea: Mythos Tales includes many of their shorter Lovecraftian fiction pieces. Kiernan is the publisher of Sirenia Digest , billed as "A Monthly Journal of the Weirdly Erotic", where much of their short fiction has first appeared since 2005.

P. Lovecraft 's " Pickman's Model ". Written by Josh Finney , illustrated by Patrick McEvoy and edited by Kat Rocha , it was published by 01Publishing in 2016; both softcover and digital download editions were released. Synopsis Arkham , 1946: amidst a spree of killings perpetrated by the Arkham Slasher, private eye Hank Flynn is hired by a wealthy woman to find missing artist Richard Upton Pickman . Interspersed with flashbacks of Flynn's wartime service, the investigation leads him to cross paths with psychics, Deep One hybrids and Ghouls , before culminating in a showdown with the murderous Pickman. Characters Henry R. Flynn , a former U. S. marine now working as a private investigator. Glynda , a woman with a deep knowledge of the occult, and Hank's love interest. Richard Upton Pickman , a former artist driven to madness and murder. May Derlith , a wealthy socialite who has an obsession with Pickman and his works. Thurber , a man driven to the verge of insanity through his historic association with Pickman.


--- China.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L37
НАЗВАНИЕ: China ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:13:23 СИМВОЛОВ: 4962 China Location Earth Type Country, region Notable Features Plateau of Leng Plateau of Tsang Inhabitants Humans Kuen-Yuin (Deathless Sorcerers) The Mighty Children Tcho-Tcho Serpent People Ghouls Madam Yi China is a region in Earth, which is also a nation-state and a civilization. In the Cthulhu Mythos , it is presented as a center of cult activity, particularly that of Cthulhu . The Plateau of Tsang and the Plateau of Leng have both been suspected of being in China. In Lovecraft's Fiction When the Louisiana cultist Castro gives Inspector Legrasse information about the Cthulhu Cult in " The Call of Cthulhu Pacific " and "double meanings in the Necronomicon Dr.

[4] Ghouls L213
In a world ruled by the Great Old Ones and their servitor races, populated by teeming masses of insane humans (the Dreamers), a dark, oppressive empire rules China, Korea, and parts of Vietnam, Burma, and Thailand. Founded by the Deathless sorcerers (Kuen-Yuin) of the Plateau of Leng, it's an empire of sane humans (the Dreamless) ruled by the Mighty Children (eldritch abominations created by the Deathless), with an elite consisting of the Deathless sorcerers, the Ophidians (aka, the Serpent People), and a military caste. They have formed an alliance with the Elder Things and the Ghouls , and are at war with those who serve Cthulhu (the Star Spawn and Deep Ones ), the hordes of Dreamers, the empire of the Tcho-Tcho, the Mi-Go aliens, and the Shoggoth. Protecting its borders is the "Interdiction", a continent-sized Elder Sign created using Deathless and Ophidian sorcery, Elder Thing technology, and pre-Dream human technology.


--- Clark Ashton Smith, The Return of the Sorcerer [Оригинал.txt].txt ---

[1] Ghouls L459
You have certainly cleared it up for me." His tone was dry and formal, as if he were repressing himself and holding back a world of unsurmisable thoughts and emotions. Somehow I felt that Carnby was more nervous and upset than ever, and also that my rendering from the Necronomicon had in some mysterious manner contributed to his perturbation. He wore a ghastly brooding expression, as if his mind were busy with some unwelcome and forbidden theme. However, seeming to collect himself, he asked me to translate another passage. This turned out to be a singular incantatory formula for the exorcism of the dead, with a ritual that involved the use of rare Arabian spices and the proper intoning of at least a hundred names of ghouls and demons. I copied it all out for Carnby, who studied it for a long time with a rapt eagerness that was more than scholarly. "That, too," he observed, "is not in Olaus Wormius." After perusing it again, he folded the paper carefully and put it away in the same drawer from which he had taken the Necronomicon That evening was one of the strangest I have ever spent.

[3] Ghouls L1099
Another interval, and then, without warning, I witnessed the awful and unexplainable disintegration of the shadow, which seemed to break gently and easily into many different shadows ere it faded from view. I hesitate to describe the manner, or specify the places, in which this singular disruption, this manifold cleavage, occurred. Simultaneously, I heard the muffled clatter of a metallic implement on the Persian rug, and a sound that was not that of a single body but of many bodies falling. Once more there was silence—a silence as of some nocturnal cemetery, when grave-diggers and ghouls are done with their macabre toil, and the dead alone remain. Drawn by that baleful mesmerism, like a somnambulist led by an unseen demon, I entered the room, I knew with a loathly prescience the sight that awaited me beyond the sill—the double heap of human segments, some of them fresh and bloody, and others already blue with beginning putrefaction and marked with earth-stains, that were mingled in abhorrent confusion on the rug.

[6] Ghoul L247
In this wise had he learned almost all that the miserly Squarvash-Yun knew, though he had not yet emptied the skull of its grisly victuals when he was swallowed up by the Oroboros Demesne. Upon awakening to find Avasquiddoc gone and the circle disturbed, Varka Zhom had leapt at the chance to search his closet. The head he recognized as an artifact of ghoul magic, and he proceeded to bolt down what remained of the overripe memories of Squarvash-Yun. Only a few morsels had remained, but Varka Zhom greedily ate them, and marveled at the upwelling of knowledge that whelmed his senses. New languages, lore and spells sprang whole into his brain, recreating as his own memory the last crumbs of Squarvash-Yun’s centuried life.

[7] Ghoul L251
New languages, lore and spells sprang whole into his brain, recreating as his own memory the last crumbs of Squarvash-Yun’s centuried life. At the climax of it, however, he discovered too late why Avasquiddoc hadn’t finished his ritual meal; for Squarvash-Yun’s last thoughts stole into Varka Zhom’s brain and became his own. I am murdered... by Avasquiddoc, he thought another man’s final thoughts. I am... dead. Not having any familiarity with ghoul sorcery, the beleaguered constables were unable to disabuse him of his fateful mistake, and couldn’t be bothered to try. Upstairs, they found a prodigy of a serpent writhing among the debris of Varka Zhom’s ransacked sanctum. The serpent’s frantic perambulations made it most difficult for them to catch, particularly when the constables who seized it realized that it was not a proper snake at all, and that it spoke——or rather, babbled.


--- Confession in Darkness.txt ---

[3] Ghouls L101
When she finally expired, he went to his mentor, Leffler, to give him the news. Leffler, despite his enviable knowledge about the Great Old Ones, Mi-Go, Deep Ones, and other creatures, was only a "theoretical" sorcerer, having given up the practice after an incident in which he summoned something that terrified and traumatised him. He was, moreover, a cannibal, and afflicted with another recurring fear: that his enemies might devour his body after death. In exchange for his teachings, Leffler made the narrator promise to stand with him when he died, arrange the funeral and the burial in graves that could never be found, and make sure that no ghouls would eat him. The narrator takes a perverse delight in revealing that he has betrayed Leffler and devoured him himself, doing so as a part of a ritual to increase his own power. After that, the narrator spent a year and a half in Scandinavia, sometimes in the form of a werewolf , sadistically hunting animals (including regular wolves) and humans. However, after being threatened by locals who might have had access to silver bullets, he gave up his lycanthropy and moved to France in human form.

John's Eve 1983 is the fourteenth issue of Robert M. Price 's long-running Lovecraftian fanzine Crypt of Cthulhu . Edited by Price, it ran to a total of 52 pages in an A5 format and was self-published in June 1983. In Other Media[] The mobile game Tokyo Afterschool Summoners features a character based on Cthugha, who takes the form of an anthropomorphic dragon. The novel Ghouls of the Miskatonic by Graham McNeill features a war veteran who summons Cthugha to Earth to put a stop to a murderous cult of Cthulhu worshipers. Along with Nyarlathotep and Hastur, Cthugha appears as one of the main characters in the Haiyore! Nyaruko-san franchise, under the name Kuuko. Cthugha appears as a demon lord in DC Comics' Arkham Asylum: Living Hell issue #6. Known as the "Arbiter of Hell", Cthugha takes the form of a squid-like entity who speaks in rhyme. The 2012 animated TV series Teenage Mutant Ninja Turtles features a creature named Cthugha, who serves a race known as the Daagon (a pastiche of the Deep Ones).

[2] Ghoul L409
Friday (April 2020-June 2024): Ed Brubaker's supernatural mystery series features a number of Lovecraftian elements, including a secret society in service to ancient forces, occult texts, and tentacled monstrosities. One Easter egg featured is a character whose car license plate registration is "HPLOV." The Ghoul Next Door (2021): an urban fantasy by Cullen Bunn and Cat Farris which tells a story of the unusual friendship between a boy and a ghoul. Taking place in Ander's Landing, Massachusetts , several nods to the Cthulhu Mythos are made, including the ghoul's name of Lavinia , her home in Armitage Cemetery , and the ghost of a long-dead witch whose familiar takes the form of a large rat with a human face and goes by the name of Miles Jenkins Minky Woodcock: The Girl Called Cthulhu : a four-issue story arc and the third in Cynthia von Buhler's Minky Woodcock series, this is a pastiche work set in World War II featuring hard boiled detective action, famous authors and occultists, the Cthulhu Mythos, and adult content. Lovecraft : features several Lovecraft adaptations by various writers and illustrators. Hack/Slash Herbert West is featured as an antagonist in one of the story arcs. Howard Lovecraft (Arcana Studios): follows a fictionalised version of Lovecraft as a child and his pet monstrosity Spot as they explore the worlds of the Cthulhu Mythos. Infestation 2 (IDW Publishing): A crossover event featuring the Elder Gods invading the multiverse.

--- Cthulhu Universalis_ Section D.txt ---

[4] Ghouls L4083
It is also said that the enchanted wood "at two places touches the lands of men... it is well that [the zoogs] cannot travel far outside the world of dream" (308-309). Also, Basil Elton, "a light-house keeper in ancient Kingsport" (317) traveled to dreamland from his lighthouse in a white ship ( White online text ) 36-42). The Vaults of Zin can be reached from the ghouls' burrows under dreamland (339) and the pit in Leng by the throne of the high priest is said to also lead down to these vaults (373); yet in the waking world the Vaults of Zin also exist, beneath the underground realm of K'n-yan in Oklahoma ( Mound online text )). For that matter, Professor Dyer suggests that the land of the Antarctic Old Ones "must indeed be the fabled nightmare Plateau of Leng which the mad author of the Necronomicon was reluctant to discuss" ( Mountains online text ) 70). Thus, Leng exists both in the waking world and dream--albeit in very different forms.

[1] Ghouls L393
They are an ancient race that is wise and powerful. Though not devout worshipers they do obey and worship Cthulhu, Dagon, and Hydra, and will sacrifice humans if commanded. Serpent Men = Only a few of them are left, usually frightened degenerate creatures. Once in a while one is born with the power and intelligence of the ancients, and will turn to human cultists for its followers and allies. It may have the power to pass as a human. Ghouls = Though aware of the Great Old Ones, they don't worship them though they will do what is necessary to keep them appeased (e. g. sacrificing humans). They also recruit into their tribes humans, often Mythos cultists. Mi-go = An alien race based in Yuggoth that has been conducting mining operations on Earth for untold eons and using humans in their mines. Forced to keep their activities underground once mankind develop technology advanced enough, they now use Mythos cultists as their liaisons and spies among humans. It's a beneficial relationship in which both the Mi-go and Mythos cultists gain.

--- Cthulhu_Universalis_Section_D_a24d23cf7d.txt ---

[2] Ghouls L939
Incl: Williams, Mr. ; Potter, Andrew; Dunlock, Wilbur. Dixon, Mark Son of Cthulhu and a human, Kay Keith, who died in childbirth. Dixon was raised by Judson Moybridge, who found him through a foster-parent program. They lived on Los Feliz Boulevard in Los Angeles. Moybridge gave the child his own mother's maiden name of Dixon. Dixon worked as a junior researcher for the Los Angeles Times. He witnessed an attempt to assassinate the Los Angeles major by a possible member of the terrorist Black Brotherhood. After a major earthquake, Dixon escaped with his girlfriend Laurel Colman through Parkland Cemetery, only to find that she had been killed by ghouls. Dixon later found Moybridge murdered and discovered that he had been working for the Black Brotherhood. Dixon was attacked by Deep Ones who drowned him in the swimming pool at Moybridge's house, but he survived the drowning. His captors then took him to the Santa Cruz mountains, where the Black Man (Reverend Nye/Nyarlathotep) showed him the Shining Trapezohedron. The light from the stone caused him to transform into Great Cthulhu, and he went forth to rule the world.

--- Cultes des Ghoules.txt ---

[2] Гулей L17
Входит в перечень пяти основных книг в "Мифах Ктулху". "Культы гулей" появляются в произведениях Кэтлин Р. Кирнан и играют особую роль в романе "Низкая красная луна" (2003).

[3] Гулей L19
"Культы гулей" упоминается как часть коллекции, которая была обнаружена в титульном замке в романе "Крепость" (1981), но не фигурирует в экранизации 1983 года, основанной на книге.

[9] Гулей L69
Рукопись копировалась и распространялась тайно с 1865 года. Роберт Блох править Роберт Блох впервые описал "Культы гулей" как книгу о черной магии, мертвецах и гулях, посвятив её главным образом парижскому культу гулей.

[11] Гулей L81
Лавкрафт править Лавкрафт часто цитировал "Культы гулей" в своих произведениях и добавлял какой-либо факт о ней, но ни разу не приводил цитат или конкретных подробностей об этом отталкивающем труде.

[12] Гулей L81
В повести "За гранью времён" Профессор Пизли из Мискатоникского университета изучал "Культы гулей", когда его сознание подменял пришелец из Великой Расы Йит.

[14] Гулей L83
Противник древних культов Лабан Шрусбери передавал её содержимое своим ученикам. "Культы гулей" не столь старинны, как многие из других запрещенных трудов, однако, история создания книги также достигла современности лишь в виде обрывочных и неподтвержденных сведений.

[15] Гулей L85
Лавкрафт упоминает "Культ пожирателей трупов Ленга" и некрофагов в рассказе "Пёс", но в поздних произведениях ссылается на "Культы гулей". Возможно, книга переняла эту роль на себя.

[16] Гулей L91
Август Дерлет править Август Дерлет впоследствии стал часто упоминать "Культы гулей" в своем творчестве. Несколько магов также сделали рукописные копии на итальянском или испанском языках.

[17] Гулей L93
Граф д’Эрлетт пишет о своей принадлежности к культу гулей и даёт ряд пророчеств относительно его будущего. Наряду с этим появляются описания языческих обрядов плодородия, посвящённых божествам земли.

[18] Гулей L95
"Культы гулей" — академический фолиант, более полезный исследователю, чем оккультисту: описываемый в нем культ гулей мало полезен; сектанты уже знают все о них и хранят эти секреты подальше от исследователей.

[21] Гулей L109
Граф д’Эрлетт был исследователем тайных книг18-го века, чей отталкивающий фолиант был опрометчивым разоблачением деятельности тайного культа Гулей, скрытого среди французской аристократии высшего сословия. Расследование д’Эрлетта привело к его смерти от их рук.

[22] Гулем L109
Расследование д’Эрлетта привело к его смерти от их рук. Граф д’Эрлетт сам был Гулем и шокирующие действия Культов Гулей в его фолиантах автобиографичны.

[1] Ghouls L13
НАЗВАНИЕ: Cultes des Goules ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:15:17 СИМВОЛОВ: 5177 Cultes des Goules Also known as Cults of Ghouls Created by François Honoré-Balfour Place of origin France Type Data Storage and Transmission Function Book Real World Information Creator Robert Bloch First Appearance CIRCLE The Suicide in the Study Other Appearances HPL The Shadow Out of Time The Haunter of the Dark CIRCLE The Shambler from the Stars The Dark Demon The Secret of Sebek AWD The Return of Hastur The Whippoorwills in the Hills The Seal of R'lyeh EXP "They Only Come Out at Night" The Encyclopedia Cthulhiana "Spindleshanks (New Orleans, 1956)" The Keeper's Companion Low Red Moon Eldritch Horror Demonbane "Lest some would label it blasphemy, I have chosen to explain certain actions and beliefs, and let God be the judge of us all." François Honoré-Balfour , Cultes des Goules Cultes des Goules , or Cults of Ghouls , is a fictional book of black magic. In the Cthulhu Mythos , it is said to have been written by François Honoré-Balfour ( Comte d'Erlette ) in 1702. It was published in France and later denounced by the Church. Only a handful of copies are in existence.

[3] Ghouls L135
Description The book, a 600-page quarto, goes into details about the existence of necromancy and necrophilia in France at the beginning of the 18 th century. According to the author, there was a society of tomb robbers and grave robbers, with various blasphemous rites and practices. Members of the society called themselves "goules". A person wishing to join the society must perform necrophagy at the initiation ceremony. Other acts include divination by reanimating the dead and necrophilia. ( EXP The Keeper's Companion The French witch covens are also mentioned, along with their ties to a secret society of subhumans ( ghouls ) inhabiting the catacombs of Paris. The Great Old One Nyogtha is also referred to frequently, as is the Outer God Shub-Niggurath , who is described as being linked to werewolves and other lycanthropes ( EXP The Keeper's Companion ). There are also tales of the Yeti EXP The Encyclopedia Cthulhiana Also included are some prophecies, a few necromancy spells and ways to contact different beings such as Nyogtha, Shub-Niggurath and her dark youngs , ghouls and byakhees . Simple phrases in ghoul's language are translated and included.

[4] Ghouls L171

Simple phrases in ghoul's language are translated and included. The Voorish Sign is also mentioned. Reading the book frequently may alter the physiology of the reader to be more ghoul-like. --- D. L. Hutchinson.txt ---

[2] Ghoul L91
Daryl and Joe created cover art for the Detroit tabloid Fun Magazine in the 1980s. Lovecraft-inspired works include the 1991 comic H. P. Lovecraft's Cthulhu: The Whisperer in Darkness , by Millennium Publishing. In 2002, Daryl worked with close friend and noted Mythos writer Stan Sargent to create cover art for Sargent’s books The Taint of Lovecraft , and Ancient Exhumations . Artwork based on the writings of Clark Ashton Smith and Robert E. Howard remain unpublished with plans for an art portfolio in the future. In 2023, Daryl worked with Oniricous lead singer and guitarist Joaquín Granero on cover art for the album Los Cultos del Ghoul , by the Spanish death metal band Oniricous, born from the ashes of GHUL. The band is devoted to the works of H. P. Lovecraft.


--- DELTA_GREEN_ALIEN_INTELLIGENCE,_edited_c44c040834.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L49
This is my official apology. I was particularly struck by Stolze's story in this anthology and it's my opinion that Stolze is a true talent. Watch for other work from this guy!) Adam Scott Glancy, one of the primary authors of Pagan's Delta Green sourcebook, contributes the next story in the anthology. What could be more evil than nasty, occult-obsessive Nazis? Mr. Glancy knows. Bob Kruger's hefty contribution to the anthology is deliciously detailed and not a page too long, even at 50-pages. Ghouls, Delta Green, the Necronomicon, rich characters, action. Mmm, mmm, good. Finally, Blair Reynolds takes a break from his artistic endeavors and delves head-first into writing. His contribution to the anthology started out just a little slow for me, but as soon as the story switched from the "debriefing" to the action, I was hooked. I'm a fan of Reynolds' art, and now of his writing. A great way to finish the anthology off with a bang and leave the readers wanting more. So, there you have it.

[1] Ghouls L219
[ HK Hunt online text )] Dr. Jean-Francois Charriere's papers included diagrams of operations designed to give a man the attributes of a reptile; some of these diagrams were attributed to De Vermis Mysteriis AWD Survivor online text )]. Saracenic Rituals The chapter called Saracenic Rituals deals with Prinn's sojourn in Egypt in the days of the Crusaders. Prinn wrote of what he learned from Alexandrian seers; of his journeys into deserts and his looting of tombs in hidden valleys of the Nile. The chapter reveals the lore of the efreet and the djinn , the secrets of the assassin sects, myths of Arabian ghouls , and the hidden practices of the dervishes. [ RB Sebek ] The chapter includes a chant relating to the efreet and djinn RB Hell online text ]. The chapter tells that the Egyptian priesthood worshiped gigantic beings who were half-beast and half-man. The priests obtained great power from these beings, but had to offer them incense and human sacrifices. The chapter speaks in particular detail of the worship of Sebek , the crocodile-headed god. [ RB Sebek The chapter tells that the pharaoh Nephren-Ka called up Nyarlathotep with the sacrifice of a hundred willing victims.

--- Dungeons & Dragons.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L105
The mind flayer , an ancient civilization of evil humanoids with cephalopod-like heads and mind control powers, could be inspired by the spawn of Cthulhu ; Gygax says he was inspired by the cover of Brian Lumley The Burrowers Beneath The ghast , an undead creature resembling a ghoul , seems to take its name from The Dream-Quest of Unknown Kadath . The lich may be influenced by the "liche" Clark Ashton Smith 's "The Stair in the Crypt," although Gygax credited Gardner Fox's "The Sword of the Sorcerer" (1969). In the 5th edition of the game, players can choose the Warlock class, which requires them to make a deal with an "otherwordly patron" to gain magical powers. One type of patron suggested is a Great Old One , with "Great Cthulhu" offered as an example.

--- Egypt.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L93
( HPL The Outsider Nitokris Queen Nitokris, a ruler of Egypt of the Sixth Dynasty (c. 2345-2181 BCE), "once invited all her enemies to a feast in a temple below the Nile, and drowned them by opening the water-gates". She was buried alive beneath the Third Pyramid, which 4,000 years later is still shunned on her account "at certain phases of the moon". She is called the " ghoul -queen", and is said to reign with her husband Khephren over the composite mummies "that are neither of man nor of beast". She is beautiful, but the right side of her face is "eaten away by rats or other ghouls". ( HPL Imprisoned With the Pharaohs The Outsider says that he finds gaiety only in the "unnamed feasts of Nitokris beneath the Great Pyramid".

[3] Ghoul L121
immortal pharaohs Nitokris and Khephren survived into the modern age and worshiped the Sphinx HPL The Outsider Khephren Khephren built the Second Pyramid sometime after 2800 BCE, and restored the statue of the Great Sphinx; its face was "probably altered to form a colossal portrait" of Khephren, "that men might look at the colossus without fear." He also built the Temple of the Sphinx, a "yawning gateway temple" in which was found a life-size diorite statue of Khephren that was moved to the Cairo Museum. It's said that Khephren still "lives far underground wedded to the ghoul-queen Nitokris and ruling over the mummies that are neither of man nor of beast." The guide Abdul Reis who abducts Harry Houdini and leaves him tied up below the pyramids is a dead ringer for Khephren. ( HPL Imprisoned With the Pharaohs Khephren is the Greek name of Khafre, a historical pharaoh of the Fourth Dynasty who actually reigned c. 2558-2532 BCE. Khafre is indeed believed to have built the Second Pyramid and to have had the face of the Great Sphinx carved in his likeness. The Sphinx A creature which may or may not be an Avatar of Nyarlathotep.

And those goons that took Shinia --" "I'm sorry, but what 'goons'?" Mariposa interrupted quietly. Mark blinked in surprise. "Didn't I tell you about them?" Mariposa shook his head, so Mark described them, in excruciating detail. Mariposa listened intently, his hand on his chin, then ruminated in silence for some minutes. Finally he nodded and said, "What you say makes logical sense. There is no reason to believe that the Deep Ones or the ghouls would be the only outré beings that miscegenate with humans. As a matter of fact, I've read about a race of creatures that live underground and look and act like moles, but are civilized and live in cities. They are often referred to as the 'Tulpans', though they serve as the basis for such legends as the Knockers of Cornwall, the Erdluitles of Switzerland, the Korred of Brittany, and the Red Caps of Scotland. They are said to infiltrate isolated human communities, giving wealth and prosperity but demanding a mixture of blood lines in payment.


--- FICTION_6ff2d8e9f0 [Оригинал [Оригинал.txt].txt].txt ---

[1] Ghoul L75
. . worshipped by the people of Khem as the God of the Night". (I'm 'translating' here, to spare you the effort of trying to read the archaic original text.) De la Poer wrote that Azrad called him 'The Whisperer' and that he gave his followers "secrets and prophesies that brought both power and doom to those who sought them". Azrad warned that he still had hidden cults around the world, despite repeated efforts to wipe them out. De la Poer echoed this idea, stating that ". . . the ghoul-cults are said to be centered in the mounts of far-away Thibet, and perhaps closer, if reports from Holland are to be believed." This made me think immediately of The Hound. Yog-Sothoth was also simple to match; Yok-Saggoth was described as "the foul primal One, the Key to Many Doors, he who walks between the Worldes." Questing further I found two names, Xuloth and Q'aath-Hulul, which sounded like Cthulhu. Xuloth was referred to as "the high priest of the Dark Gods," however, from the references I could not be sure if he was supposed to be a god, demigod, or human.


--- FICTION_805dfd0093 [Оригинал [Оригинал.txt].txt].txt ---

[5] Ghoul L117
His voice was little more than a cracked, hoarse whisper and although apparently thin as to be fragile, his body was solid as an oak. Vicious, claw-like nails grew from his fingers, he was deceptively agile as well as superhumanly strong. And merciless. He was known as the Ghoul, no one knew who he had been or what he had been called but to him these details were part of another, different life. One which had ended screaming in Kingsport, leaving only the vengeful Ghoul behind. A revenant of anger unabated. Tomorrow was Halloween, he grinned, and he would commemorate the anniversary in the blood of the man he hated the most.

Nothingness, an end to everything. Peace at last. There was a lot of blood on his hands and he could not find solace in himself or in blaming Oakes.

He pulled out his razors and began to twirl them expertly in his hands, lashing out here and there to heighten the cultist's fear.

[2] Ghouls L67
The conclusion of the story reveals that the man, and all those who work in the division, is slowly devolving into one of the creatures due to a supernatural aura which seems to infect those in proximity to them. Characters Professor Gordon Craig , a former primatologist and head of the Special Subway Division for 25 years. Mythos Connections Gordon refers to the stretch of subway haunted by the ghouls as a "mad Nyarlathotep -world". Gordon also tells of how he learned much from H. P. Lovecraft , and vice versa, implying that this fictionalised version of Lovecraft had somehow come into possession of knowledge on the subject. Possible Inspiration A possible inspiration for the story is an image described in " Pickman's Model ", painted by the titular character . This image shows a pack of ghouls attacking a subway station: a similar event happens in "Far Below" when the survivors of a train crash are fed upon by a number of the beasts.

--- Gerald Canevin.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L203
This is never mentioned again in the series. In fact, the novella Seven Turns in a Hangman's Rope is a complete rewrite of "West India Lights" that uses exactly the same plot, but makes no mention of Gertrude or any other love interest. A recurring character in the Canevin series is Dr. Pelletier , a navy surgeon who provides scientific commentary on the fantastic events experienced while also showcasing a knowledgeable and open-minded attitude towards the supernatural. Spirits, deities elementals ghouls magic rituals and psychic emanations are all real in the series, although Canevin, reflecting Whitehead's status as an ordained minister, expresses his belief that the power of God exceeds all of these, which is demonstrated by the successful use of exorcisms and holy water to dispel some of the apparitions ( CIRCLE : "The Black Beast", "Mrs. Lorriquer"). Canevin's family is from Virginia. His father was Alexander Canevin. ( CIRCLE : "The Chadbourne Episode") Personality Canevin is a jovial man with many friends, and is very knowledgeable about occult matters. He is generally brave and level-headed, although he admits to being "fussy" about his belongings. Another aspect of his personality is that he always speaks the truth, even when he concedes that he shouldn't. He hates waking up early, but always attends church. He also gets along well with children.


--- Ghast.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L89
Their language appears to consist of "coughing gutturals". They also appear to travel in packs, as Randolph Carter meets a group of fifteen. Together they seemingly manage to bring down a Gug. Ghasts may be encountered in places where sunlight never comes, such as underworld labyrinths, cavern networks, and deep tunnel systems. They shun light and are fearful of the sun, as when they are exposed to direct sunlight they sicken and may eventually die. Like the ghouls , the ghasts have sharp hooves for feet, although they are larger and move around by hopping. Their faces are eerily humanoid, but have neither a nose nor a forehead. Often living in groups of 10 to 20 members, some ghast communities can accommodate up to 60 or more individuals. Ghasts prefer to go about either singly or in pairs but may hunt in larger packs, making the best use of their knowledge of underground networks to herd and trap prey.


--- Ghasts_(Cthulhu_Universalis)_198587615e.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L19
Источник (URL): Название: Ghasts (Cthulhu Universalis) Автор: Unknown Источник данных: live-сайт Символов: 1334 Сохранено: 2026-08-25T21:57:33+03:00 SHA-256 контента: 17248fae36516950462db33e7b2881adb3c4f1fa6c4b3d41be26ebcb4fe6edb1 ТЕКСТ Ghasts [HPL Kadath (online text)]: Of the dreamlands. Primitive creatures who live in the pitch-dark Vaults of Zin, which connects to the underground realm of the gugs. The ghasts are the main food of the gugs, who enter the Vaults of Zin to hunt them. In turn, the ghasts creep forth during the sleeping-hour of the gugs to attack them while they are defenseless. The ghasts cannot discriminate among victims, and so they attack ghouls as readily as gugs, and even eat each other. Light is fatal to the ghasts, though they can tolerate the twilight of the gugs' kingdom for hours. Their sense of smell is very sharp. Ghasts are the size of a small horse. Their aspect is scabrous and unwholesome, filthy and disproportioned. Their faces are curiously human despite the absence of a nose, a forehead, and other important particulars. They have yellowish-red eyes. They have long hind legs and hard pointed hooves. They move by grubbing about or making kangaroo leaps. They speak in coughing gutterals.


--- Ghorii.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L75
They have long muzzles and double rows of teeth, and have a savage bite. They run on all limbs, but can use their taloned forelimbs for grasping. Their sole source of moisture is the blood of their victims, so they devour both the living and the dead, including fallen Ghorii. Ghorii arose in the desert of Zothique and like Ghouls may once have been living humans. However it is also possible for a powerful sorcerer to use magic to create Ghorii from jackal bones and animal skins. Their general appearance is broadly similar to a desert version of the Ghoul. They are usually the color of the local soil, and hide very well, except for the stench which reveals their presence. They prefer to travel in large bands or hordes pursuing prey including intelligent beings such as the humans of Zothique relentlessly and ravenously tearing them to pieces if they catch them.

Hence, ghouls are not knowledgeable of the geography of upper dreamland. Masters and Allies The ghouls have no masters, and do not owe allegiance to the Other Gods or Nyarlathotep. However, the Other Gods can control the ghouls when they must.

[24] Ghouls L45
However, the Other Gods can control the ghouls when they must. The ghouls are bound by solemn treaties with the night-gaunts.

[25] Ghouls L47
However, the Other Gods can control the ghouls when they must. The ghouls are bound by solemn treaties with the night-gaunts. The ghouls and night-gaunts communicate by means of ugly gestures.

[33] Ghouls L55
After arriving at Kadath, the ghouls and night-gaunts disappeared; apparently either banished or destroyed by the might of Nyarlathotep and the Other Gods.

[38] Ghoul L63
The cat-goddess Bubastis was a type of ghoul, also known as Chewer of Corpses [RB Brood].

--- Graveyard Rats.txt ---

[9] Ghoul L315
And before the fireplace, up and down, up and down, scampered a creature with red eyes, that squeaked and squealed—a great grey rat, maddened by its failure to reach the flesh its ghoulish hunger craved. Saul Wilkinson began to laugh—horrible, soul-shaking shrieks that mingled with the squealing of the grey ghoul. Saul's body rocked to and fro, and the laughter turned to insane weeping, that gave way in turn to hideous screams that echoed through the old house and brought the sleepers out of their sleep. They were the screams of a madman.

[15] Ghouls L1461
Harrison yelled and heaved convulsively, and at his movement the rats gave back in alarm. But they did not leave the grave; they massed solidly along the walls, their eyes glittering redly. Harrison knew he could have been senseless only a few seconds. Otherwise, these grey ghouls would have already stripped the living flesh from his bones—as they had ripped the dead flesh from the head of the man on whose coffin he lay. Already his body was stinging in a score of places, and his clothing was damp with his own blood. Cursing, he started to rise—and a chill of panic shot through him!

[16] Ghoul L1493
Harrison fought down a mad wave of terror. He would not withdraw his hand unless he could lift his body from the coffin lid—and the imprisonment of his hand held him prostrate there. Trapped! In a murdered man's grave, his hand locked in the coffin of a headless corpse, with a thousand grey ghoul-rats ready to tear the flesh from his living frame! As if sensing his helplessness, the rats swarmed upon him. Harrison fought for his life, like a man in a nightmare. He kicked, he yelled, he cursed, he smote them with the heavy six-shooter he still clutched in his hand.


--- Gugs_(Cthulhu_Universalis)_c3184bef24 [Gugs (Cthulhu Universalis).txt].txt ---

[1] Ghouls L19
Источник (URL): Название: Gugs (Cthulhu Universalis) Автор: Unknown Источник данных: live-сайт Символов: 2395 Сохранено: 2026-08-25T21:57:16+03:00 SHA-256 контента: f28b8782510c1531a699fd1c75551cd3039a439e594e02ccd98b1aec784b128b ТЕКСТ Gugs [HPL Kadath (online text)]: Of the dreamlands. The gugs are hairy and gigantic beings, twenty feet in height. A buried gug will feed a community of ghouls for almost a year. Its arm is covered with black fur and bifurcates into two short forearms, with a paw each. Each paw is two and a half feet across, with formidable talons. The head is as large as a barrel, with two pink eyes that jut two inches from each side, shaded by bony protuberances overgrown with coarse hairs. The mouth runs vertically from the top to the bottom of the head, and has great yellow fangs. The gugs have no voice, but talk by means of facial expression.

[2] Ghouls L23
Each paw is two and a half feet across, with formidable talons. The head is as large as a barrel, with two pink eyes that jut two inches from each side, shaded by bony protuberances overgrown with coarse hairs. The mouth runs vertically from the top to the bottom of the head, and has great yellow fangs. The gugs have no voice, but talk by means of facial expression. However, their hearing is very sharp, and they have grown accustomed to seeing without light when necessary. The terrible kingdom of the gugs intervenes between the gulf of the ghouls and the enchanted wood. A great wall separates the ghouls from the gugs' kingdom. The gugs' city extends through their whole kingdom. The city has round towers of immense height, with thirty foot doorways. The gugs once lived in the enchanted wood, where they reared stone circles and made sacrifices to the Other Gods and the crawling chaos Nyarlathotep. Then an abomination of theirs reached the ears of the Great Ones, who banished them to caverns below.

[3] Ghouls L23

However, their hearing is very sharp, and they have grown accustomed to seeing without light when necessary. [4] Ghouls L29
Near the Tower of Koth is a cemetery and also the mouth of the vaults of Zin. Formerly, the gugs ate mortal dreamers for their food, and they have legends of the toothsomeness of such dreamers. But since their exile to the caverns, their diet is restricted to the ghasts. The gugs venture into the vaults of Zin to hunt ghasts in the dark. However, during the gugs' hour of rest, the ghasts sneak out of the vaults of Zin to attack the sleeping gugs. The gugs are somewhat afraid of ghouls, but would still attack the latter if circumstances were sufficiently advantageous. The night-gaunts planned to feed the conquered moon-things to gugs and other creatures of the Great Abyss. The gugs can be summoned to any place that someone has set up stones and said the forbidden words three times [AWD Lurker, Whippoorwills].


--- H. P. Lovecraft's Book of Yog-Sothothery.txt ---

[8] Ghouls L125
Lovecraft's Entities Lovecraft populated his short stories and novellas with some truly bizarre gods and monsters ... and now so can you. Each entity has game statistics appropriate to the role they're likely to play in a game ( Great Old Ones have just a few numeric stats, but lesser creatures are fully statted). Azathoth Beings of Ib Bholes Bokrug Cats of the Dreamlands, Colour Out Of Space , Creeping Corpses, Great Cthulhu Dagon and Hydra Deep Ones Elder Things , Formless Hunter, Ghasts Ghouls Gnorri , The God of the Sunken Temple, Great Race of Yith Gugs Hastur , Hounds of Death, Hypnos Keziah Mason King in Yellow Leng Folk Mi-Go Moon Beasts Night-gaunts Nodens Nyarlathotep , Polyp Horrors, Rat Things , Resurrected Abominations, Resurrected Corpses Shantaks Shoggoths Shub-Niggurath Spiders From Leng Star Spawn , Winged Servant, Yog-Sothoth Zoogs Lovecraft's Cults Groups who swear loyalty to the unnatural forces of the universe are rarer in Lovecraft's writings than in many later Mythos authors, but several important cults and cult-like societies are mentioned. No sane human being would wish to be part of such an order, but as adversaries for a chilling scenario, such weird groups can add significant color and menace.


--- H. P. Lovecraft, The Hound [Оригинал.txt].txt ---

[1] Ghouls L27
There were nauseous musical instruments, stringed, brass, and wood-wind, on which St. John and I sometimes produced dissonances of exquisite morbidity and cacodaemoniacal ghastliness; whilst in a multitude of inlaid ebony cabinets reposed the most incredible and unimaginable variety of tomb-loot ever assembled by human madness and perversity. It is of this loot in particular that I must not speak—thank God I had the courage to destroy it long before I thought of destroying myself. The predatory excursions on which we collected our unmentionable treasures were always artistically memorable events. We were no vulgar ghouls, but worked only under certain conditions of mood, landscape, environment, weather, season, and moonlight. These pastimes were to us the most exquisite form of aesthetic expression, and we gave their details a fastidious technical care. An inappropriate hour, a jarring lighting effect, or a clumsy manipulation of the damp sod, would almost totally destroy for us that ecstatic titillation which followed the exhumation of some ominous, grinning secret of the earth. Our quest for novel scenes and piquant conditions was feverish and insatiate—St.

--- HORRORS_BEYOND_edited_1e865febbf.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L95
As a consequence this collection is of direct interest to fans of mythos fiction. The short version of my opinion is that this is a terrific book and all mythos fans should read it! "The Eyes of Howard Curlix" by Tim Curran -- I have read one other story by Tim Curran, "The Chattering of Tiny Teeth" in Warfear, a delightfully creepy exploration of ghouls residing in No Man's Land during WWI. This new story concerns a tabloid journalist who interviews a scientist who has found a way to perceive the worlds that we cannot perceive because our vision is limited to a small fraction of the electromagnetic spectrum, and how our perception of what dwells there allows it to perceive us. This was a very well written tale, a great way to kick off the anthology. No mythosian entities but an overt nod to Abdul Alhazred.

--- Hplfic4_7f0bf63579 [Оригинал [Оригинал.txt].txt].txt ---

[1] Ghouls L59
A most impressive set of pine shelving occupied the bulk of one wall and I approached it. Several of the shelves had crumbled onto those below and volumes once lodged there were heaped in fetid rotting piles on the floor, deteriorated beyond separation into distinct books. Avoiding those piles, I drew closer to the intact shelves and perused the lettering on the spines that had not succumbed to the decay. As it was in French for the most part, I translated automatically, guessing at the archaic forms and certain proper names: The Cults of the Ghouls, The Pleasures of Death and The Dying, Formulas of Possession, Ancient Mysteries, The Nameless Order, Of Mngthyl's Powers, The Theory of . . . some noun, unintelligible although frankish in appearance. There were others of astounding suggestion and frightening intimation; the words "death," "secrets," "gods" (always plural) and others too hideous to recall danced before my tired eyes as the whispered fragments of the Vrinny Legend throbbed in my ears, louder than the interminable rain. I shuddered and swept my light around.


--- Hyborian Age.txt ---

[3] Ghouls L307
In The Shadow Out of Time , by H. P. Lovecraft, a Cimmerian chieftain from 15,000 BC is one of the captive minds that Nathaniel Peaslee meets after being forcibly mind-swapped with a member of the Great Race of Yith On Howard's part, some elements of the Hyborian Age might have been derived from the Mythos. For example, the Ghouls that attack Conan in The Hour of the Dragon resemble those from Lovecraft's " Pickman's Model " and Clark Ashton Smith's " The Nameless Offspring ", complete with dog-like jaws. In the very first Hyborian story, "The Phoenix on the Sword", the images of "half-forgotten gods" carved in the walls of Epemitreus' tomb within Mount Golamira are referred to as "the Nameless Old Ones". The implied connection to the Mythos was actually explicit in the first draft of the story, in which the passage specifically mentioned " Cthulhu Tsathoggua , Yog-Sothoth, and the Nameless Old Ones".

[5] Ghouls L401
The similar name "Dagoth" appears in "The Scarlet Citadel", which tells us that the half-human wizard Tsotha-lanti was born from a woman impregnated by a monster when she slept "too near the pre-human ruins on Dagoth Hill". A location named Dagoth Hills is featured in " The Dwellers Under the Tombs ", which takes place in modern day America rather than the Hyborian Age. In this story, the Dagoth Hills are inhabited by subterranean creatures which share traits with both the Little People and the Ghouls : both species known to impregnate human females to father hybrid offspring ( ADJ The Novel of the Black Seal CIRCLE The Children of the Night The Nameless Offspring Possible Influences Many elements of the Hyborian Age are paralleled in the Mythos. "Shadows in the Moonlight" mentions ancient gods which mated with human women in the past, much like the Great Ones in Lovecraft's The Dream-Quest of Unknown Kadath , or Yog-Sothoth in his " The Dunwich Horror ". Olivia refers to these ancient gods as the "nameless, forgotten ones", and claims that they have since "gone back into the still waters of the lakes, the quiet hearts of the hills, the gulfs beyond the stars".


--- In the Vale of Pnath.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L81
Using a crystal to contact the great sorcerer Maal Dweb, from the world of Xiccarph , Eibon learns of a mage named Shuggob who might be able to help him. Eibon travels to the Dreamlands and reaches the shadowy Vale of Pnath , where he meets Shuggob, a scholarly ghoul who lives in a stone house and offers him a meal that might have been made from human flesh. When Eibon asks about Glund fluid, Shuggob tells him that he has a cauldron of it brewing at the moment, and offers some. Inside the cauldron there is a swollen, wrinkled grey mass with hooked knives buried into it, causing it to produce a slimy fluid. To Eibon's shock, he realises that the thing is the still-living brain of some undisclosed creature. The horrified wizard runs away, never to visit the Vale of Pnath again.


--- Innsmouth Essays.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L73
Running to a total of 154 pages, it was published in a paperback format by Innsmouth Gold in September 2022. Overview This book gathers together a collection of twelve essays from the first year or so of Innsmouth News Innsmouth News is the in-house fanzine of the Innsmouth Book Club podcast, a show which covers all aspects of Lovecraftian and weird fiction, film, gaming and art. The essays, all written by Robert Poyton, cover a wide range of topics. From Lovecraftian sites in the UK, to the Roots of Cthulhu. From Tolkien's use of class, to screen depictions of Robert E Howard Conan the Barbarian There's a look at Deep Ones ghouls , Lovecraft's use of music, the Howardian Mythos, HPL on TV and more. All rounded off by a guide to Lovecraft's Providence , and an essay from the Old Gent himself on writing weird fiction. --- Jim Culver.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L75
He made his debut appearance in the Dunwich Horror expansion for the second edition of Arkham Horror Overview A young black man from New Orleans, Jim Culver inherited a trumpet from his father, one which has caused him no end of trouble: despite being a talented trumpeter, playing the instrument allows him to commune with the dead, and even, on occasion, raise them to life. He also appears to be accepted by the graveyard ghouls , and has been known to exchange information with them. Behind the Mythos Since his debut, Culver has appeared in several Fantasy Flight releases, including Eldritch Horror Horrific Journeys expansion), Elder Sign and Arkham Horror: The Card Game . In 2010 a pre-painted plastic miniature of the character was released as part of the Arkham Horror Premium Figures line, but this has since been discontinued. Trivia Culver made a cameo appearance in Graham McNeill 's novel Ghouls of the Miskatonic , in which he and Marie Lambeau performed a set at a speakeasy attended by two of the main characters.

--- John Henry Booth.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L85
He prefers to dress in the weather beaten attire of a New Arkham Ranger or those of a cattle rancher. John is a former Ranger that led Gamma Squadron for the Council of Leaders in New Arkham. He spent many years traveling and charting the Wasteland outside of his city-state in hopes of helping humanity's pockets of survivors. Unlike the majority of his fellow Rangers, John held little animus to mutants or members of human-related races like the Deep Ones or Ghouls John was raised as the son of Marcus Booth and Winifred Booth. When he was a boy, his father murdered his mother and attempted to kill him before being killed by John in return. The reason for this was becasue Marcus Booth had discovered John was not his biological son. He was in fact conceived by an unknown inhuman father (at the behest of magician-scientist, Alan Ward). John would eventually marry a psychic Loyalty Officer, Martha Booth, and had two children, Gabriel and Anita.

[3] Ghoul L99
Cthulhu Armageddon EXP John wakes up imprisoned and sentenced to death in his hometown of New Arkham. His teammates, Gamma Squad, have been killed and he has no memory of how it happened. Interrogator Mercury Takahashi helps him escape in exchange for taking him with her. John has no interest in helping her, though, and plans to gain revenge on whoever is responsible for his teammates' death. Journeying to the frontier town of Scrapyard, they encounter the ghoul Richard Jameson who helps John explore the Dreamlands . This leads him to discover one of his fellow Rangers, Jessica O'Reilly, is alive. It also leads him to discover that the person who killed his squad, Alan Ward, is leading the Cult of Cthulhu to kidnap local children. Seeking out the Black Cathedral, John eventually allies with local tribals from Dunwich to assault it.


--- Joseph Curwen.txt ---

[3] Ghouls L115
Whereas their ultimate goals in the novel are more ambiguous, in the film it is revealed that Curwen and his associates are actually working to bring about the return of Yog-Sothoth, Cthulhu , and other ancient gods. Behind the Mythos The ultimate goal of Curwen and his allies' activities, i. e. the nature or the use for the information extracted from the resurrected wise persons, is not completely specified, and its interpretation is largely left to the reader. The closest thing to a description of Curwen's aims is contained in a passage describing the ashes central to his experiments: Could it be possible that here lay the mortal relics of half the titan thinkers of all the ages; snatched by supreme ghouls from crypts where the world thought them safe, and subject to the beck and call of madmen who sought to drain their knowledge for some still wilder end whose ultimate effect would concern, as poor Charles had hinted in his frantic note, "all civilisation, all natural law, perhaps even the fate of the solar system and the universe"? This ambiguity also affects the exact circumstances of Curwen's "first" death. It is evident he was betrayed and probably killed by the entity summoned in his defense during the siege to the hidden grounds of his farm, but the identity of this being, as well as its possible connection with Yog-Sothoth (whose name is mentioned in the incantations), is left open to speculation.

--- Kate Winthrop.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L73
A researcher in the physics department at Miskatonic University during the late 1920s, Kate's life changed when an experiment ripped a hole in reality, allowing a terrifying abomination through which consumed her partner, the unfortunate Professor Young. Since then she has devoted her time to creating a weapon with which to combat the creatures she now know knows lurk just out of sight. In Graham McNeill 's novel Ghouls of the Miskatonic , Kate appears as a minor character who conducts research on a mysterious artefact uncovered by the investigators, but achieves little other than destroying her laboratory. Behind the Mythos Since her debut in Arkham Horror Second Edition, Kate Winthrop has appeared in many of Fantasy Flight's Arkham Horror Files releases including Eldritch Horror and Elder Sign . A pre-painted plastic miniature of the character was also released as part of the Arkham Horror Premium Figures line. Trivia In the original flavour text for Arkham Horror , Kate developed her device at Miskatonic University. In Eldritch Horror Arkham's counterpart on the global scene, she conducted her research at the University of Buenos Aires.


--- Khephren.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L115
In Lovecraft's Fiction According to Lovecraft ( HPL Imprisoned with the Pharaohs Khephren built the Second Pyramid shortly after 2800 BCE. He restored (rather than constructed) the statue of the Great Sphinx; its face was "probably altered to form a colossal portrait" of Khephren, "that men might look at the colossus without fear." He also built the Temple of the Sphinx, a "yawning gateway temple" in which was found a life-size diorite statue of Khephren that was moved to the Cairo Museum. It's said that Khephren still "lives far underground wedded to the ghoul-queen Nitokris and ruling over the mummies that are neither of man nor of beast." On one occasion, he posed as a tourist guide named Abdul Reis el Drogman, and under that identity kidnapped Harry Houdini to see if the famed magician's magic tricks could escape more ancient traps than those of modern men.


--- Labyrinth.txt ---

[1] Ghouls L63
It is set in the popular Arkham Horror Fiction universe. Plot Summary Joe reaches the library on campus and when entering he is greeted by the librarian Daisy Walker. She had been saved from cultists and ghouls by Joe and in her gratitude had helped Joe get information from the library whenever he needed as it was usually restricted to faculty and students. Joe tells her he needs information and she brings him to a table lit by a single candle. He shows her the pictures of the woman and the body, and Daisy says she has seen something similar to the birthmark but doesn’t believe the victims are cultists and not the same cultists who attacked her. She disappears amongst the bookshelves and when she returns she shows him a leather-bound tome titled "The Labyrinth: The True Tale of the Darkness Below Crete".

--- Martian.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L119
By removing certain glands and enzymes, they eliminated all their emotions except fear (which was deemed necessary for survival), and made themselves biologically immortal and incapable of aging. By eradicating all pathogenic bacteria and sterilising their planet, they no longer had to worry about disease. Without religion, they dedicated their lives to intellectual and aesthetic pursuits, which included building labyrinthine and Cyclopean structures to express aesthetic, mathematical, or metaphysical formulae. They explored other worlds and traded with other civilisations, such as the Mi-Go . Eventually, the Martians discovered an ancient species of long-faced ghoul -like creatures called the Elder Gods , which were even more advanced than them, and used to be the dominant species on Mars before their arrival. Finding that these god-like precursors weren't dead, but merely sleeping, the Martians gave in to fear, abandoned their planet, and tried to conquer the Earth in 1898. ( EXP To Mars and Providence Trivia Frank Belknap Long also wrote about tentacled Martians who invaded Earth in his short story "The Blue Earthman" (1935). However, Long's Martians differ from Wells' Martians in being gigantic (capable of holding humans with the tip of their tentacles) and need to touch each other's foreheads to communicate, as opposed to being actual telepaths.

--- Mordiggian.txt ---

[1] Ghoul L15
НАЗВАНИЕ: Mordiggian ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:25:36 СИМВОЛОВ: 1297 Mordiggian Also known as The Charnel God The Great Ghoul Lord of Zul-Bha-Sair Morddoth First appearance ADJ The Charnel God Appearances CIRCLE The Dark Eidolon Created by Clark Ashton Smith Mordiggian is a fictional deity created by Clark Ashton Smith for his Zothique story " The Charnel God ". The entity was later incorporated into the Cthulhu Mythos as a Great Old One by Scott D. Aniolowski in his Call of Cthulhu roleplaying game. Described as swirling darkness, a gigantic shadow, or a worm-shaped column, Mordiggian is worshipped by several human cults and cults of ghouls as a symbol of Death. His name is used in Zothique, Man's last continent, and it is possible that this gigantic creature is the same as Shaurash-Ho or even identical to Cynothoglys .

[3] Ghoul L89
His name is used in Zothique, Man's last continent, and it is possible that this gigantic creature is the same as Shaurash-Ho or even identical to Cynothoglys . The name may also find its origins in medieval France, being a corruption of the Latin "Mortician" (to do with the dead). There is also at least some faint echo of the Celtic death goddess The Morrigan in the name, especially since one of her epithets was The Crow That Feasts On The Battlefield. Mordiggian also seems to be the main subject of The Ghoul's Manuscript and Cultes des Goules In the far future time in which Zothique stories take place, the ghoul-like Ghorii most likely worship Mordiggian as their ghoul-god. (source?)
